tbmm-2017-07-26sit01spe0003par0029	Thank you, Mr. Gürer. The second non-responsive statement belongs to Mersin MP Ali Cumhur Taşkın, who wants to talk about fighting forest fires. Here you go, Mr. Excessive.
tbmm-2017-07-26sit01spe0004par0034	Mr. President, dear lawmakers, I have taken an off-the-book commitment to combat forest fires. I salute the General Assembly. As it is known, with the beginning of summer every year, there is a significant increase in forest fires in our country, and because of these fires, our forest assets in our country's lungs have suffered severe economic and ecological losses. Forest fires are at the top of the threats to the forests. Forest fires destroy not only trees, but all living and lifeless creatures living in the forest. It's not just the trees that we've lost, but thousands of species of plants and animals with trees, so a living ecosystem is dying. Every year there are 4 million hectares of forest burning in the Mediterranean Belt, 550,000 hectares. In our country, 8.9,000 hectares of forest have been damaged. Since our country is located in the Mediterranean climate belt, 65 percent of forest fires emerge in our Mediterranean and Aegean coastal region. Twelve and a half million hectares of our forest areas, nearly half of them, are at risk of high fire. In our country, 87 percent of the annual forest fires are human origins, and 84 percent are neglect and carelessness, 3% are intentional and 13 percent natural. In the last 15 years, our government of the forest has made significant progress in combating forest fires and is doing well. Mr. President, valuable lawmakers, three basic strategies are being followed in combating forest fires. First, training and awareness programmes are being conducted to prevent a fire, and second, early warning and effective struggles are being carried out in case of fire, and third, burned, damaged areas are being wooded within a year, except for the purpose of burning areas. Our forests are monitored 24 hours from 776 towers for detection of forest fires and early warning. 1,075 first responders have been deployed in the various locations of our forests for the purpose of early fire intervention. In combating and putting out forest fires, 19,718 personnel are employed, using 2,372 vehicles and business machines, including five amphibious aircraft, 24 water-throwing helicopters and 6 administrative helicopters. In the three days between July 30th and July 2nd, 147 fires were fired and successfully extinguished without growth. Compared to forest fires around the world and forest fires in our country, it's clear how successful we are in fighting forest fires. According to the last decade's data, Spain has an average of 91,000 hectares per year, 75,000 hectares per year in Portugal, 75,000 hectares in Italy, 45,000 hectares in Greece, while in our country it is only 8.9,000 hectares. In this area, we are the most successful country in our region. In the wake of this result, the innovative moves made by our forest agency in the fire management system, infrastructure and technology investments, along with indoor training, have been effective in the self - sacrificing efforts of forest headquarters personnel. So far, 112 martyrs have been made during the fire suppression. I ask God's mercy on this occasion for all our martyrs. Mr. President, the esteemed MPs, of course, protect the forests, not the guards. I specifically ask our citizens to conduct their picnics in picnic areas, not toss out cigarette butts while traveling through the forest without putting out any smokes, to call ALO 177 and 110 lines that are free when they see any smoke. As I put an end to my words, I salute the General Director of the Forest, especially Mr. Forest and Water Affairs, who has been conducting the work of fighting forest fires for 24/7, to all of our members of the Forest Service, to all of our forest villagers who have supported us, to all who have done the work, and to all who have done the work, I salute the General Assembly.

Payday workers are the class where they have nothing but their hard work. In order to live their lives, they rent their labor force to the capital class, which holds the production tools in their hands. Therefore, individual workers are at a disadvantage in negotiating against the members of the capital class. Workers come together to fight this asymmetry, organized in unions and labor troops. The history of labor struggles and gains is the history of these troops, developed in a way against the capital class. If the state is a social contract between citizens, as described in general, and all citizens are paid workers and paid workers are unemployed, it is expected that public authorities will protect the rights and freedoms of workers in the interests of the employer. Third, freedom of expression for an MP means that he has to express the subject in his own way, in his own way, in his terminology. Dear MPs, of course, these views of freedom of expression that we're talking about do not belong to us alone. On the other hand, it is possible to see the most contemporary practices of lawmakers' freedom of expression in countries that represent the important example of pluralistic democracies. According to the European Commission for Democracy, which makes assessments of freedoms of expression, the principle of non-responsibility is an additional assurance to lawmakers in the face of a majority opinion expressed within parliament. As representatives of the people who put themselves in that position, lawmakers represent some of the national sovereignty, even if they have minority thoughts. Respect for national sovereignty, which belongs to the people, is the centre of the majority democratic principle. Thus, the true function of legislative immunity is the expression of common will and the establishment of parliamentary composition as elected by citizens. Again, I salute the General Assembly.

The Interior Charter Exchange Proposal we are talking about envisions resolving problems stemming from the implementation of the Parliamentary General Assembly to ensure effective, efficient, and peaceful implementation of legislation and control activities in the process. In this framework, there are regulations in the Civil Charter, such as revisiting and extending the hours of some talks. Dear MPs, the Grand National Assembly of Turkey is a veteran parliament that has carried out the guarantees of our national state, the capacities of national will and the struggle against imperialism. It is, of course, normal to be in the Veteran Parliament, symbolizing such an important past and vital importance, to place some responsibilities on the MPs, and it is a requirement of the rule of law. In Article 95 of our Constitution, it has been stated that the Turkish Grand National Assembly will conduct its work according to its internal ordinances, while lawmakers' rights and obligations are undoubtedly in accordance with constitutional order. It must not be forgotten that our Constitution begins as the Constitution, which defines the eternal existence of the Turkish homeland and nation and the undivisable integrity of the Great Turkish State. One of the aims of these amendments is to prevent the history and common history of the Turkish nation, which has been guaranteed since the beginning of the Constitution, and to insult and abuse the constitutional order established in the first four Articles of the Constitution, to prevent it from making definitions contrary to the administrative structure of the Republic of Turkey's country and its non-divisible integrity in the Constitution, in order to do so. Again, the 81st article of our Constitution, as it may be remembered, has concluded the oaths of lawmakers as they go into office, and the third article of the Civil Code states that the oath will be read aloud from the bench. In summary, the oath is to pledge honor and honor in front of the great Turkish nation about the existence and independence of the state, the indivisible integrity of the country and the nation, the sovereignty of the nation, the secular and democratic order and loyalty to the Constitution. An MP elected by the Turkish nation in the Republic of Turkey and within the framework of constitutional order would never be allowed to prevent himself from reading the text of the oath in an approach denying his elected state, his nation, the Constitution, and all national values. Therefore, the amendment adds that lawmakers will begin their duties under oath, while lawmakers who prevent oaths will not be able to benefit from rights stemming from the capacity of MPs. In short, with the amendment to the Civil Code, Turkey's Grand National Assembly is being shaped with an understanding based on its work and efficiency, providing a transparent and peaceful working environment, providing the fulfillment of legislation and control activities to the extent that our beloved nation expects, and on the other hand, the members of the Gazi Assembly, which conducts the Turkish nation's struggle for independence, are intended to act in accordance with the state of the Turkish Parliament as a veteran of the Grand National Assembly and in accordance with our Constitution. Dear lawmakers, it is up to us to say that these regulations are not in order to be removed from the original framework of the content of the Civil Code amendment. In fact, it is intended to change the Civil Code to become a Parliament that will not be a place to deal with problems, but a parliament that will solve problems. Basically, the basis of the arrangements for the efficiency and efficiency of parliament's work is the framework of understanding provided by the Compromise Commission established in the Grand National Assembly of Turkey, which started with this acceptance in the 23rd and 24th term. While those who object to the amendment claim that the right to say has been restricted, the periods that have been focused on the work of the 24th term are even shorter than the times prescribed in the provisions. The General Assembly is also extending its working hours, and with the removal of the elements that have caused the waste of time in the Parliament, which has provided more efficient and efficient work, it is possible for both opposition and power to focus on the fundamental issues, and the work that our nation is waiting for will be done quickly. Dear MPs, the most objectionable part of the proposal is the regulation of the 15th article. In this sentence, it is a punishment for the President, the President of Turkey, the President, the Vice President of the Presidential Court, the MP, the Turkish nation's history, and the joint history of the Turkish nation, to insult and abuse the constitutional order established in the first four Articles of the Constitution, to violate the administrative structure of the Republic of Turkey, and its non-divisible integrity with the country and the nation of Turkey. However, for what reason, however, is the punishment of the defamation of the great Parliament being condemned, or insulting the basic principles of our republic, the founder of our republic, Mustafa Kemal Ataturk, to speak divisively against the unity and integrity of our state and nation, to accuse the Turkish nation of genocide? Should the objection be regarded as an inconvenience to the secular, democratic, social law state of our republic, or should it be seen as a sign of dissatisfaction with the uninterrupted integrity of the Turkish nation and its homeland and the administrative structure of Turkey in the Constitution? Is there anyone under this blessed roof who can't digest the foundation principles of our state, the basic qualities of our republic, the unitary national structure of the Turkish state? In our view, this arrangement refers to our history and our common history, to the establishment principles of our state, to the administrative structure defined in the Constitution, to the unity of our nation and to the dissolution of the country, to our national values, and to the republic's founder, Gazi Mustafa Kemal Ataturk.
